NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, January 8, 2019
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, January 8, 2019, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order:  Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to 5
minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: December 11, 2018  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 December 2018  
 
c. Clerk of Superior Court Report November 2018  
 
d. Juvenile Probation Report November 2018  
 
e. Constable Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1,November; Winslow Precinct #2, November;
Snowflake Precinct #3, November & December, 2018 
 
 
f. Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, Winslow Precinct #2, Snowflake Precinct #3,
Kayenta Precinct #4, Show Low Precinct #5, Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6 November 2018
 
 
g. Personnel Actions  
 
h. Appointment of Natalie Stobs to the Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development
Board (NEAZ LWDB)
 
 
i. Northeastern Arizona Workforce Development Plan Modification

<!-- page 2 -->
j. Revised fee schedule for the Assessor's Office  
 
k. Approval of 21 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount of $7,154.04  
 
l. Reissuance of Stale-Dated Checks  
 
m. 2019 Board Committee Appointments  
 
n. Contract signed by County Manager per Board of Supervisors Resolution: Contract
Amendment Number 2, Contract for Uniform Rental and Laundering Services, #B15-12-010
 
 
o. Authorization for the County Manager to send notification of intent to cancel current Indigent
Defense contracts with outside attorneys and transition to a new annual contract model for these
services
 
 
3. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Intergovernmental Agreement, contract number ADHS19-214846 between Navajo County
Public Health Services District and Arizona Department of Health Services to perform child
death reviews for Navajo and Apache Counties July 1, 2018 through June 30, 2023
 
 
2. Intergovernmental Agreement, contract number CTR040469 with Arizona Department of
Health Services to perform STD prevention, testing, counseling and surveillance activities
throughout Navajo County effective January 1, 2019 through December 31, 2023
 
 
3. 2018 Wildland Fire Hazardous Fuels - Cooperative Forestry Hazardous Fuels Program, Grant
Agreement WFHF 18-204
 
 
4. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Financial Update and FY2020 Budget Development Plan  
Presented By: Bryan Layton
 
5. NORTHEASTERN ARIZONA INNOVATIVE WORKFORCE SOLUTIONS:  
 
a. Consideration of formation of the Northeastern Arizona Community Resource Network as a
501(c)3 Nonprofit that will be operated through Navajo County.
 
Presented By: Stephanie Ray
 
b. Consideration of authorization to enter lease negotiations with the City of Show Low for a
portion of the building located at 760 E. McNeil in Show Low, Arizona.
 
Presented By: Stephanie Ray
 
c. Consideration of authorization for Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board,

<!-- page 3 -->
c. Consideration of authorization for Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board,
in partnership with Northern Arizona Council of Governments Community Services
Administration, to apply for Community Services Block Grant funding.
 
Presented By: Stephanie Ray
 
6. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and Board
members.
 
 
 
7. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: January 4, 2019 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 10:30 a.m. By: MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Title: LWDB Appointment Representing Apache County

Motion before the Board:
Appointment of Natalie Stobs to the Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board
(NEAZ LWDB)
Background:
Workforce Development Board members are appointed to a 3-year term.   The appointment of
Natalie Stobs is to fill a vacant Apache County Business seat and will be in effect through July 1,

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.i.        
Meeting Date: 01/08/2019  
Title: NEAZ LWDB Plan Modification-Final Draft
Submitted By: Carla Fails, Operations Manager
Department: Northeastern Az Innovative Workforce
Motion before the Board:
Northeastern Arizona Workforce Development Plan Modification
Background:
Pursuant to WIOA regulations, each Local Workforce Development area was required to modify
their four-year plan before the end of the second year.  This plan encompasses the three local
areas of Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties, as well as the One-Stop Partners.

Equal Opportunity Employer/Program 
Auxiliary aids and services available upon request to individuals with disabilities  
Section 2:  Executive Summary 
 
The Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Area is comprised of Navajo, Apache, 
and Gila Counties.  Our local rural area encompasses over 25,973 square miles in the northeast 
corner of Arizona. The area is geographically diverse, ranging from a desert climate in the 
southern regions to high desert and mountains, including the White Mountains, Arizona’s 
second-highest mountain range. The population of the Northeastern Arizona local area is 
234,063.   The Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Area is a three-county Local 
Workforce Development Area and is part of the American Job Center national network. 
 
The Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act (WIOA) was signed into law on July 22, 2014 
and became effective July 1, 2015.    The Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act of 2014 
supersedes the Workforce Investment Act of 1998 and amends the Adult Education and Family 
Literacy Act, the Wagner-Peyser Act, and the Rehabilitation Act of 1973.   The Workforce 
Innovation and Opportunity Act is designed to help youth, job seekers and businesses.   The 
Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act requires each Local Workforce Development Area 
to develop and submit to the Governor a comprehensive four-year local plan, in partnership 
with the Chief Elected Officials. 
 
An INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT for Navajo, Apache and Gila County WIOA Partnership 
was fully executed and effective on July 1, 2015.    The AGREEMENT was executed to form a 
single recognized entity to administer and operate programs  under Title I of the Federal 
Workforce Investment Act of 1998 and the newly enacted Federal Workforce Innovation and 
Opportunity Act of 2014.   The AGREEMENT designated Navajo County Board of Supervisors to 
serve in the role of Chief Elected Official, fiscal agent, grant recipient, and administrative entity.   
 
A new INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT has been fully executed effective January 1, 2017 
that continues to designate Navajo County to serve in the roles of Chief Elected Official, fiscal 
agent, grant recipient, and administrative entity.   
 
The Workforce Arizona Council envisions a job-driven public workforce development system in 
Arizona that focuses equally on the employment needs of job seekers and employers’ needs for 
skilled labor with industry-recognized credentials to meet their current and future demand for 
labor.   The Workforce Arizona Council has identified twelve (12) characteristics that the Local 
Workforce Development Board embraces: 
 
1. Extensive Knowledge of Local Economy 
2. Business Engagement 
3. Sector Partnerships 
4. Work-Based Learning Opportunities 
5. Job Seekers Trained for In-Demand Jobs 
6. Industry-Recognized Credentials

8. Access to Services

12. Use Evidence-Based, Best and Promising Practices for Service Delivery 
 
Business and community partnerships will be an important strategy in the Local Workforce 
Development Area.  The Local Arizona Workforce Development Board sector analysis will focus 
on new and emerging industries and occupations.

The Board’s strategic vision for preparing an educated and skilled workforce inclusive of youth 
and individuals with barriers to employment in order to support the local area economic 
growth and economic self-sufficiency is to build economic success for business and workers 
through workforce development partnerships. 
The Local Workforce Development Board Mission statement is to facilitate business retention 
and growth by partnering with education, service agencies and the business community to 
empower individuals with skills that promote self-sufficiency. 
1. Create a local workforce system that is relevant to business customers through dynamic 
partner alignment and integration, and by building on collaborative initiatives in each of 
the region’s population centers.

population center to align business services with the unique needs of employers in each

opportunities to grow the talent pipeline in in-demand and emerging industries and 
occupations

SECTION 3:  ANALYSIS 
 
a. Analysis of Regional Economic Conditions 
 
i. Counties covered; brief description of the characteristics of the local area and list of service access 
sites: 
 
The Northeastern Arizona Local Arizona Workforce Development Area includes the non- tribal 
lands throughout Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties.   The Local Workforce Development Board 
has named the local workforce system ARIZONA@WORK Northeastern Arizona. 
 
The Local Workforce Development Area is comprised of a geographically diverse region located in 
northeast Arizona.   The region encompasses 25,973 square miles with a population of 234,063 
people, 178,568 of whom are over the age of 16.  The central portion of the area consists of high 
desert grasslands, while the northern and southern areas have high mountains, including the 
White Mountains, which is Arizona’s second highest mountain range. 
 
Educational attainment in the Local Workforce Development Area varies from the statewide 
statistics. Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties all have a higher rate of high school graduates 
(includes equivalency) than the state; however, there is also a higher percentage of individuals 
who have not completed high school. Post- secondary attainment is lower than the rest of the 
state.  
 
The unemployment rate in Navajo, Apache, and Gila showed a steady decrease from 2011 to 2016; 
however, from 2016 to 2018 unemployment rates remained steady in Gila County and rose slightly 
in Apache and Navajo Counties. Additionally, the rates for all three counties (7.1% in Navajo, 9.7% 
in Apache, and 5.8% in Gila) are significantly higher than the state unemployment rate of 4.9%.  
 
Although unemployment rates are higher across the state for individuals with no more than a high 
school diploma, the unemployment rates in Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties are markedly higher 
than the state. The largest disparity occurs in Navajo a nd Apache Counties, where the 
unemployment rate of those with a high school diploma  is nearly double the state rate (22.5% in 
Navajo County and 21.8% in Apache County vs. 11.0% statewide.  
 
The Local Workforce Development Board has one Comprehensive ARIZONA@WORK Job Center in 
Globe, Arizona and affiliate ARIZONA@WORK Job Centers in Show Low, Payson, Winslow, and 
Eagar. 
 
The Local Workforce Development Board has identified a number of industry sectors that are 
critical to the economic growth of the region. The Local Workforce Development Board is 
comprised of representatives from economic development agencies as well as the local business

community. The targeted industry sectors were determined based on knowledge of current and 
expected industry trends, a review of the regional economic development strategies, as well as a 
thorough assessment of the current labor market statistics report provided by the State of Arizona 
Office of Economic Opportunity (OEO)

Northeastern Arizona In-Demand Occupations were identified as occupations with high recent 
and projected annual growth. They are broken down by industry and detailed in this analysis 
under Section 3 A.iii. 
 
Recent economic development that impacts sector selection and occupations in demand: 
 
Manufacturing, which includes our lumber and bio-energy plants, is a thriving industry in 
Navajo and Apache Counties. During the past two years,  area leadership has successfully 
collaborated with Congressional Representatives and forest industry workgroups to have more 
access to and expand the use of Forest Service land. According to the Economic Development 
Directors in our region, most of the site selectors looking at relocating to our area are 
representing agriculture production companies. These companies will require skilled employees 
trained in advanced manufacturing processes to install and maintain production equipment. 
 
There are three power plants in the Local Workforce Development Area. Combined, they are 
the largest employers in Apache County and the third largest in Navajo County. While they are 
not showing an increase in the number of new jobs in the statistics provided by OEO, it is 
expected that a large number of employees will be retiring and that new employees will need 
to be hired and trained. With the advent of new technology within the energy industry, 
employees may need to receive incumbent worker training to maintain their current positions.

<!-- page 402 -->
10 
Equal Opportunity Employer/Program 
Auxiliary aids and services available upon request to individuals with disabilities  
The Health Care Industry is projected to have employment gains of 1.2% during the 2017-2019 
timeframe according to the OEO data. Based on recent and in-progress hospital expansions in 
the area, it is anticipated that employer demand for skilled employees in all in-demand health 
care occupations will be much more substantial. Cobre Valley Regional Medical Center in Gila 
County completed a 66,810 sq. ft. expansion project in Fall 2016 which increased the number of 
inpatient beds in multiple departments and also included a new pharmacy, cath lab, wound 
center, and physician offices. Summit Regional Medical Center in Navajo County is in the midst 
of an $80,000,000  expansion project that is scheduled to be completed in May, 2019. The 
project includes an outpatient center that will house multiple specialty physicians and increase 
the types of services available to area residents. Physicians moving into both the Gila County 
and Navajo County areas will require skilled front and back office staff including trained Medical 
Assistants, Certified Nursing Assistants, Registered Nurses, and Medical Secretaries. 
The Local Workforce Development Board has determined that the industry and occupational 
focus will be in the following sectors:

NAICS 23  Construction 
 
NAICS 22  Utilities 
 
NAICS 32  Manufacturing 
 
NAICS 48  Transportation 
 
 
Due to the unique nature of our 3-county region, and the wide variety of economic and industry 
characteristics that exist from one geographic area to another, the Local Workforce 
Development Board has made the decision to focus on providing training and career pathway 
opportunities within the 5 in-demand industries but has given staff the ability to use discretion 
in providing services to participants who have a clearly defined career pathway outside of the 
in-demand industries. In addition, the LWDB clarifies that there are a variety of support 
occupations, i.e., office and IT support, that cross industry boundaries and are considered viable 
career pathways for the purpose of providing training and supportive services.

Navajo and Apache – Designated as a high poverty area for Youth 
 
Youth living in high poverty areas are automatically considered low-income individuals. WIOA 
defines a high poverty area is a census tract, set of continuous census tracts, Indian reservation, 
tribal land, or Native Alaskan Village or county that has a poverty rate of at least 30 percent as 
set every five (5) years using the American Community Survey 5-year data. Two counties in 
Arizona, Navajo and Apache Counties, have been identified with a poverty rate higher than 30 
percent.  
b. An analysis of the knowledge and skills needed to meet the employment needs of 
the employers in the region, including employment needs in in-demand industry 
sectors and occupations (an existing analysis may be used, per 108(c) of WIOA); 
 
Basic Skills: There is a recognized high demand for GED preparation services and remedial 
education in the Local Workforce Development Area and is often the first step for participants 
seeking employment along the career pathways in the targeted industries.  Within the In-
Demand industry sectors, Adult Education will play a large role in providing services for job 
seekers to meet the needs of the employer.  
 
Soft Skills: There is a recognized need for training in soft skills as job postings in the region 
indicate employers are specifically looking for employees who exercise proper workplace 
etiquette. The Arizona Career Readiness Credential plays an important role in providing career 
readiness awareness training to job seekers in Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties. 
Supervisory Skills: Many of the Occupations In-Demand for the targeted industries are First-
Line Supervisory level positions. This is a skill-set area that has potential for incumbent worker 
training and could be used as part of an up-skill, back-fill strategy. 
 
Career and Technical Education (CTE):  Many of the In-Demand Occupations in the targeted 
industries in the Local Workforce Development Area require industry recognized credentials 
and vocational skills. Training providers in the area work closely with employers to continually 
update program offerings to coincide with current employer and industry needs. 
 
Industrial Skilled Trades: It is anticipated that as Baby Boomers in the energy industry continue 
to retire, there will be an increased need to fill the talent pipeline in Energy and Industrial 
Maintenance and Operations occupations.  
 
Skilled Construction Trades: According to the data provided by OEO, the Construction industry 
is one of the fastest-growing industries in the region. There will be a continued need for 
employees skilled in various construction trades. 
 
Healthcare Trades: The most recent OEO analysis identified high demand for healthcare 
practitioners at all levels – entry level, technician and professionals.

Meeting the workforce demands of the business community requires an alignment of economic 
development, education (including post-secondary), and workforce development efforts in the 
local LWDA strategies.   The Local Workforce Development Area will participate in monitoring 
ongoing assessments of not only the state’s current economic environment, but the county’s 
contribution to it as well. It will be critical during the lifespan of this plan to closely monitor the 
economic direction of the county’s business attraction efforts. Targeted industries identified by 
the county along with current in-demand industries and the occupations within those industries 
are the drivers for the workforce activities. 
 
The Northeastern Arizona LWDB works in partnership with the Economic Development entities 
in the region including REAL AZ Economic Development Council, NACOG Economic 
Development Council as well as the various municipal economic development departments. 
This partnership ensures a constant flow of information to and from the workforce board about 
the current state of industry and the workforce needs of the region’s employers.  
 
In addition to regular updates to the LMI provided by state entities such as the Office of 
Economic Opportunity used to develop the Local Workforce Development Plan, the LWDB 
convenes regular meetings with employers and training provider partners to gather real-time 
information that is used to inform decisions regarding in-demand industries as well as the 
expenditure of funds to create workforce pipelines in career pathways otherwise identified to 
benefit both the employers and job seekers in our 3-county region.

employment (and unemployment) data, and information on labor market trends, 
and the educational and skill levels of the workforce in the region, including 
individuals with barriers in employment:

indicates, from 2016 to 2018 unemployment rates remained steady in Gila County and rose 
slightly in Apache and Navajo Counties. Additionally, the rates for all three counties (7.1% in 
Navajo, 9.7% in Apache, and 5.8% in Gila) are significantly higher than the state unemployment 
rate of 4.9%.

d. An analysis of the workforce development activities (including education and 
training) in the region, including an analysis of the strengths and weaknesses of such 
services, and the capacity to provide such services, to address the identified 
education and skill needs of the workforce and the employment needs of employers 
in the region. 
 
Given the high percentage of residents in Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties who lack a high 
school diploma (or its equivalent), adult basic education is a priority in the region. Northland 
Pioneer College is the Adult Basic Education provider in Navajo and Apache Counties, while the 
Gila County Educational Service Agency provides these services in Gila County.  The Local 
Workforce Development Area works closely with the adult basic education providers to support 
participants in earning their General Education Diploma (GED).  
 
Northland Pioneer College and Gila Community College play an important role in providing 
post-secondary training needed within the Local Workforce Development Area, offering areas 
of study to gain the skills, knowledge, or certification needed to begin or enhance a career 
forecasted to be an in-demand industry or occupation.  Both colleges currently offer trainings 
for the following in-demand health care occupations: Registered Nurses, Medical Assistants, 
and Medical Secretaries. These community colleges also work with local employers to 
provide clinical experience for the students. 
 
Northland Pioneer College offers   Energy and Industrial Technician certificate and degree 
options which prepare graduates for various occupations within the Manufacturing and Utilities 
industries. 
 
For participants seeking training for in-demand occupations in the transportation industry, 
participants will be guided to the Eligible Training Provider List to find a training provider that 
will provide the skills, knowledge and certification needed. 
 
There is a need to coordinate resources to expand on-the-job training and Registered 
Apprenticeship programs and accelerate the pipeline of qualified workers to open positions in 
Construction and Manufacturing in-demand occupations and the Local Workforce Development 
Board will work closely with local employers and the Arizona State Office of Apprenticeship to 
design and implement trainings not only for positions that do not require formal training but 
will also work to enhance on-the-job training opportunities to ensure that students completing

<!-- page 410 -->

approved training programs have access to additional employer-specific training and local 
employment. 
 
The Local Workforce Development Board has worked with the educational institutions in the 
three-county area to build the skills and knowledge of jobseekers.    Like all rural areas, 
Northeastern Arizona is limited by the number of available certificate and/or credential 
opportunities both stackable and portable.  Any program of study on the Arizona Job 
Connection Eligible Training Provider List as well as On-the-Job Training, and Apprenticeships 
can be offered to gain the skills, knowledge, or certification needed to begin or enhance a 
career forecasted to be an in-demand industry or occupation. The Local Workforce 
Development Board’s focus on specific industries and occupations will provide a blueprint for 
creation of credentials and certifications available through local training providers.

Section 4:  Goals and Strategies 
a. A description of the local board’s strategic vision and goals for preparing an 
educated and skilled workforce (including youth and individuals with barriers to 
employment), including goals relating to the performance accountability measures 
based on primary indicators of performance described in section 116(b)(2)(A) in 
order to support regional economic growth and economic self-sufficiency; and,  
The Local Workforce Development Board’s strategic vision for preparing an educated and 
skilled workforce inclusive of youth and individuals with barriers to employment to support the 
local area economic growth and economic self-sufficiency is: 
To build economic success for business and workers through workforce 
development partnerships. 
The Local Workforce Development Board Mission Statement is: 
 Facilitate business retention and growth by partnering with education, 
service agencies and the business community to empower individuals with 
skills that promote self-sufficiency 
Strategic Priorities and Goals: 
1. Create a local workforce system that is relevant to business customers through dynamic 
partner alignment and integration, and by building on collaborative initiatives in each of 
the region’s population centers. 
 
Goal 1: Implement a Business Services team throughout Northeastern Arizona and 
enhance collaboration with community partners to develop formal coalitions in each 
population center to align business services with the unique needs of employers in each 
community. 
 
2. Establish career pathway models in industries that have sustainable wage and career 
opportunities to grow the talent pipeline in in-demand and emerging industries and 
occupations  
 
Goal 1: Develop a Youth Program Design framework that includes an outreach and 
marketing plan, career pathway models for in-demand and emerging industries and 
occupations, partnerships with community-based organizations that serve young adults 
with barriers to employment, and a youth participant framework tool for 
ARIZONA@WORK partner staff.

<!-- page 412 -->
20 
Equal Opportunity Employer/Program 
Auxiliary aids and services available upon request to individuals with disabilities  
Goal 2: Build on collaborative program partnerships with Northland Pioneer College and 
Gila Community College and develop short-term programming that allows job seekers to 
quickly reach the “first step” on a career pathway by earning a credential in less time than 
traditional post-secondary semesters or school years. 
 
3. Implement customer-centered processes and technology to enhance customer flow 
through the Job Centers and between partners and track outcomes to ensure that the 
ARIZONA@WORK Northeastern Arizona One-Stop System is effectively serving job 
seekers with barriers to employment. 
 
Goal 1: Implement Common Welcome, Intake, and Customer Flow Processes within all 
ARIZONA@WORK Northeastern Arizona Job Centers  
 
Goal 2: Design and Implement a Professional Development Plan for all ARIZONA@WORK 
Northeastern Partner staff 
 
Goal 3: Implement a technology system that enhances communication, referrals, and data 
tracking throughout the ARIZONA@WORK Northeastern Arizona One-Stop System 
 
b. Taking into account analyses described in subparagraphs (A) through (D), a 
strategy to work with the entities that carry out the core programs to align resources 
available to the local area, to achieve the strategic vision and goals described in 
subparagraph (E). 
 
The Local Workforce Development Board has established a team comprised of core program 
partners to provide recommendations and advice on the local workforce system and to 
establish procedures for aligning resources.    The team has discussed aligned procedures that 
will enhance the Local Workforce Development Area’s ability to carry out the vision and goals 
for the ARIZONA@WORK System. These procedures  are designed to ensure that ALL jobseeker 
clients, regardless of where they first access the workforce system, will be registered in the 
ARIZONA@WORK database.

Section 5:  Workforce System and 
Partners 
a. A description of the workforce development system in the local area that identifies 
the programs that are included in that system and how the local board will work 
with the entities carrying out core programs and other workforce development 
programs to support alignment to provide services.   Discuss how the LWDA will 
align polices, operations, administrative systems and other procedures to assure 
coordination and avoid duplication of the workforce programs and activities.   
Describe agreements and procedures in place or in progress, and describe how the 
local board will work towards WIOA compliance in regard to the selection of a one-
stop operator and a funding mechanism for one-stop infrastructure costs (see WIOA 
sec 121 and draft regulations Part 678).   Also include a link to where policies and 
procedures are posted and provide copies of existing agreements in the appendix.   
 
The Local Workforce Development Area is a three-county area including Navajo, Apache, and 
Gila Counties.  The Local Workforce Development Area is fortunate to have had long standing 
relationships with the core and required partners identified in the Workforce Innovation and 
Opportunity Act and a history of working together for the good of the communities they serve.   
The Local Workforce Development Area is named Northeastern Arizona Innovative Workforce 
Solutions and is part of the American Job Center national network. 
The Board of Supervisors in the three-counties designated the Navajo County as fiscal agent, 
grant recipient, and administrative entity for the Local Workforce Development Area.  
In June 2015 an Intergovernmental Agreement setting forth the fiscal relationships between 
Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties under the Local Workforce Development Area was 
approved.  This agreement provided that Navajo County is designated by Navajo County, 
Apache County, and Gila County  Board of Supervisors to serve as Chief Elected Official, fiscal 
agent, and administrative entity for Northeastern Arizona Innovative Workforce Solutions 
(Appendix B). 
 
In November 2016, a Partnership Agreement was created between the Chief Elected Official 
and the Local Workforce Development Board.  The Local Workforce Development Board has 
been designated in accordance with the requirement in the Workforce Innovation and 
Opportunity Act to specify the relationship between the Chief Elected Official and the Local 
Workforce Development Board (Appendix C).  
 
In November 2016, a draft Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) was submitted to the 
Workforce Arizona Council.   This MOU purpose is to improve the accessibility and quality of 
services made available to a partner shared customer pool (Appendix D).

<!-- page 416 -->
24 
Equal Opportunity Employer/Program 
Auxiliary aids and services available upon request to individuals with disabilities  
Navajo County, as Chief Elected Official, appointed a diverse group based on the 
recommendations received from Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties to serve on the Local 
Workforce Development Board that is representative of business and industry (particularly 
existing and emerging in-demand industries and occupations), core program partners union and 
other community-based organizations with experience assisting individuals with barriers to find 
employment, and educational leaders.   Core program partners, required partners, and other 
community-based organizations serve on the partnering group that works with the One-Stop 
Operator to advise the Board on issues associated with alignment and integration of services.       
The Board has one standing committee: 
1. Executive Committee shall meet as necessary between scheduled meetings of the entire 
Local Workforce Development Board membership and shall be responsible for interpreting 
and putting into effect the decisions, policies and programs of the Local Workforce 
Development Board.  If a Local Workforce Development Board meeting has been cancelled 
and action items are needed, the Executive Committee shall put into effect and vote on the 
action items.  All actions are subject to ratification of the full Local Workforce Development 
Board at its next regularly scheduled meeting. 
 
The Northeastern Arizona Workforce Development Board in partnership with the Chief 
Elected Official selected Navajo County to serve as the Title I Adult, Dislocated Worker, and 
Youth Provider. The Executive Director functionally reports to the Northeastern Arizona 
Workforce Development Board and formally reports to the County Manager for Navajo 
County. The Executive Director serves as support to the Northeastern Arizona Workforce 
Development Board. Details are provided within the local plan regarding arms-length 
decision-making and assurance that no conflict of interest will be real or perceived in the 
procurement of a one-stop operator or related to monitoring and oversight of programs.All 
core partners and required partners who provide services in the Local Workforce Development 
Area are part of the local workforce system.  Funding streams that represent core and required 
partners include:    
● Older Americans Act 
Services older adults through Senior Community Service Employment Program with employment and 
training assistance.  The Arizona Agency on Aging is the provider in Gila County while NACOG is 
the provider in Navajo and Apache Counties. 
● Title IB Adult Program 
Services target individuals who are 18 and older and who have been chronically unemployed or 
underemployed, have barriers to getting and keeping a job, and need assistance with job 
search and/or training.  Navajo County is the current service provider. 
● Title IB Dislocated Worker Program 
Services target individuals who have lost their jobs due to closure or downsizing through no fault of 
their own.  Navajo County is the current service provider. 
● Title IB Youth Program 
Services for youth 14 years to 24 years of age who have barriers to getting and keeping a job and need 
educational and employment services. Navajo County is the current service provider.

<!-- page 417 -->
25 
Equal Opportunity Employer/Program 
Auxiliary aids and services available upon request to individuals with disabilities  
● Title II Adult Education and Literacy 
Services target individuals in need of basic education classes with low basic skills and low educational 
attainment; English language acquisition for eligible learners to achieve competence in reading, 
writing, speaking and comprehension of the English language; and math skills needed to attain 
a high school credential and transition to post-secondary education and employment.    Gila 
County Educational Service Agency and Northland Pioneer College are the current service 
providers.    
● Title III Wagner Peyser 
Services target individuals who need help with updating or developing a resume and labor exchange 
assistance that connects them to employment.  Arizona Department of Economic Security is 
the service provider.  
● Title IV Rehabilitation Act 
Services target individuals with disabilities and as per the WIOA will be treated mainstream when 
accessing the American Job Centers.  Arizona Department of Economic Security is the provider.   
● Trade Act 
Services individuals who are eligible workers who lose their jobs, or whose hours of work and wages 
are reduced, because of increased imports or production transfers abroad.  Arizona 
Department of Economic Security is the provider. 
● Unemployment Compensation 
Services individuals who are unemployed and eligible to receive unemployment compensation.  
Arizona Department of Economic Security is the provider.    
● Veterans Program (Jobs for Veterans State Grant Program) 
Services individuals who are veterans with significant barriers to employment to receive tailored 
employment and training services.  Arizona Department of Economic Security is the provider. 
Funding streams not available in the local area include:    
● Employment and Training Housing and Urban Development 
● Migrant and Seasonal Farmworkers 
● Second Chance Act 
● Youth Build 
 
The Local Workforce Development Area embraces the concepts outlined in Arizona’s State Plan 
regarding alignment and integration.  The Northeastern Arizona LWDB has convened a variety of 
stakeholders including Economic Development leaders, regional business leaders, nonprofit 
organizations, and training providers to identify local needs and modify the local workforce 
development plan to encompass strategies and action steps to meet those needs most pressing 
in our region. To better leverage support for workforce development activities, the LWDB is 
currently partnering with the Alliance of Arizona Nonprofits to develop a 3-year project, bringing 
Americorps Vista Members to the region to build the capacity of local stakeholders to leverage 
additional funding and better support workforce development activities throughout Navajo, 
Apache, and Gila Counties.  
 
 The group has been tasked with aligning and integrating services to provide a comprehensive 
approach to serving business customers, job seekers, and youth.  This plan outlines strategies

The Boards of Supervisors in Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties, in their Intergovernmental

administrative entity responsible for the disbursal of grant funds.

The Local Workforce Development Board is not incorporated.   Navajo County was designated 
the Chief Elected Official, fiscal agent, grant recipient, and administrative entity in the 
Intergovernmental Agreement.  In its capacity as administrative entity, Navajo County serves as 
support to the Local Workforce Development Board and assists the Board in fulfilling its duties. 
Navajo County Department of Procurement is responsible for the procurement of goods and 
services in accordance with the Navajo County Procurement Code, Arizona Revised Statutes 
and Federal law.   In line with the Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act, the Local 
Workforce Development Board, the Chief Elected Official, and by virtue of approval of this Plan, 
the Governor of Arizona, have selected and approved Navajo County to serve as service 
provider for the Title I Adult, Dislocated Workers, and Youth Programs.     
d. A description of the roles and resource contributions of the one-stop partners. 
 
The Local Workforce Development Board is dedicated to a fully integrated and efficient Job 
Center Service Delivery System.  The Comprehensive ARIZONA@WORK Job Center partners 
have negotiated and signed a Memorandum of Understanding and attached Infrastructure 
Funding Agreement along with required partners that includes roles and resource contributions 
to achieve their goal of seamless services. 
 
Partners offer a variety of programs and services that can and should be combined to meet 
individual customers’ unique needs and employment readiness.  Customers enter the 
ARIZONA@WORK System (with various needs) at varying degrees of job readiness for 
employment and therefore require different programs and services based on their individual 
needs.  ARIZONA@WORK System partners will work together to provide staff, programs, and 
resources in a manner that enables customers to be provided with seamless delivery of services 
regardless of funding stream.

Auxiliary aids and services available upon request to individuals with disabilities  
The Local Workforce Development Board intends to align with Governor Doug Ducey’s vision 
for Arizona, to build a pro-growth economy that provides opportunity for all and creates 
prosperous communities. This means ensuring that all Navajo, Apache, and Gila County citizens 
eligible to work, regardless of their circumstance, background, social status or zip code, have 
access to the best economic opportunity, educational options, healthcare services, safety 
system and overall quality of life as possible. 
 
The Local Workforce Development Board realizes that different populations present unique 
challenges. The Core Partners which consist of WIOA Title l Adult, Dislocated Worker Program, 
Title ll Adult Education, Title lll Employment Services, Title lV Vocational Rehabilitation, and the 
ARIZONA@WORK System are each strategically positioned to serve a very broad audience; this 
allows the ARIZONA@WORK Job Centers to serve a wide variety of individuals with programs 
and knowledgeable staff targeted to overcome their specific barriers to employment.  
 
In the Job Centers the Welcome Team will continue to conduct the initial interview triage to 
determine how customers’ needs are best met and will make appropriate referrals to core 
partners and community resources.  Core Partner staff will be cross-trained so that all will have 
a good understanding of services available for specific populations and a referral process will be 
established.  
 
In compliance with Workforce Innovative and Opportunity Act regulations Board staff worked 
with Navajo County Procurement Department to develop the Request for Proposal (RFP) a 
complete a competitive procurement for a One-Stop Operator. Goodwill of Central and 
Northern Arizona has been awarded the contract and is acting as the One Stop Operator (20 
CFR 678.620),  coordinating the service delivery of  ARIZONA@WORK Job Center partners and 
service providers.  
 
Title IB Workforce Development Programs include the Adult, Dislocated Worker, and Youth 
Programs. The Adult Program provides adults age 18 and older a variety of workforce activities 
designed to increase employment, retention, earnings, and attainment of recognized 
postsecondary credentials. The Dislocated Worker Program provides services to adults age 18 
and older who have been terminated, laid off, or have received notice of termination or layoff 
from employment (through no fault of their own) generally due to closures or downsizing. Self-
employed individuals and individuals who meet the WIOA definition of a displaced homemaker 
may also be eligible for services. These services include basic career services, individualized 
career services and training services. Priority of Service for the Adult Program for individualized 
career and training services includes veterans, recipients of public assistance, low income 
individuals, and individuals who are basic skills deficient. 
 
The Title lB Youth Program provides services to in-school youth (Ages 14-21) and Out-of- 
School Youth (ages 16-24) with barriers to employment such as being school dropouts, subjects 
of the adult justice system, basic skills deficient, English Language Learners, pregnant and 
parenting, and/or youth with a disability. Seventy-five percent of all youth funds must be used 
to support out-of-school youth.

<!-- page 420 -->

Equal Opportunity Employer/Program 
Auxiliary aids and services available upon request to individuals with disabilities  
 
Title ll Adult Education under WIOA is to enable local adult education providers, as core 
partners of the ARIZONA@WORK Workforce System, to develop, implement and improve adult 
education and literacy services throughout the state. Individuals eligible for Adult Education 
services are those individuals who are 16 years old or older, are not enrolled or required to be 
enrolled in school, are basic skills deficient, lack a secondary diploma, or are English language 
learners. 
 
Title lll Employment Service provides a network of public employment offices that offer 
placement services for jobseekers and labor force recruitment services for employers. 
Coordination of service delivery and information sharing will offer better customer service and 
avoid duplication of services.  Title lll and Title l will share the same statewide database. 
 
Title lV Vocational Rehabilitation (VR), authorized by Congress through the Rehabilitation Act of 
1973, as amended, is now part of the Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act.  The purpose 
of the Vocational Rehabilitation program is to assist individuals with disabilities to prepare for, 
obtain, advance, and maintain competitive, integrated employment.

concerning, maximizing coordination of services provided by the State employment 
service under the Wagner-Peyser Act 929 U.S.C. 49 et seq.) and services provided in 
the local area through the one-stop delivery system, to improve service delivery and 
avoid duplication of services. 
 
Wagner-Peyser is a core program partner in the ARIZONA@WORK Job Center Delivery System 
and management has been actively involved in the partner work done to prepare this plan, 
service map, identify areas of collaboration, and build day-to-day operational procedures.    
Wagner-Peyser will be co-located full-time in the Comprehensive ARIZONA@WORK Job Center 
and share in the facility costs.   In addition, a representative will set office hours in our affiliate 
ARIZONA@WORK Job Centers. 
Along with the other core program partners, Wagner-Peyser has agreed to the customer flow 
that provides for a “no wrong door” approach to serving clients in the Local Workforce 
Development Area.   The design has all core program partner clients registering in the 
ARIZONA@WORK database and viewing a system orientation as well as receiving any basic 
career services available at the location they access.    Wagner-Peyser has also agreed to the 
referral process and initial screening to identify potential services for all Center clients.    
Wagner-Peyser, along with partner staff, will continue to provide front-door coverage, resource 
room coverage, and basic career service workshops open to all workforce system clients.    
Wagner-Peyser will participate on the local integrated Business Services Team to ensure 
employers are served according to a customer center design and that partners are not 
duplicating efforts.

f. Adult Education.   A description of how the local board will coordinate workforce 
investment activities carried out under this title in the local area with the provision 
of adult education and literacy activities under title II in the local area, including a 
description of how the local board will carry out, consistent with subparagraphs (A) 
(B)(i) of section 107(d)(11) and section 232, the review of local applications 
submitted under title II. 
 
Adult Education and Literacy is a core program partner and has been actively participating in 
the partner convening groups held in the Local Workforce Development Area to prepare this 
plan and service map, identify areas of collaboration, and build day-to-day operational 
procedures.  
The Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act focuses on targeted populations with more 
barriers than previous legislations.    The Local Workforce Development Area is expecting to 
“meet people where they are” and help individuals gain the skills and knowledge they need to 
get and keep a job.    The sector focused approach provides a blueprint for career pathways.    
Individuals will enter the system with the partner that they will most benefit from and begin a 
path toward self-sufficiency via career pathways.   The career pathways model will create a 
talent pipeline to fill employment in industries and occupations that are forecasted to have 
growth and provide a self-sufficient wage over the next few years.   The Local Workforce 
Development Board believes that many of the workforce system individuals will start with Adult 
Basic Education or English as a Second Language and build from there.   The partnership with 
Adult Basic Education and Literacy is essential.     
Along with the other core program partners, Adult Education and Literacy in all three (3) 
counties has agreed to the customer flow that provides for a “no wrong door” approach to 
serving individuals in the Local Workforce Development Area.   The design has all core program 
partner clients registering in the ARIZONA@WORK database and viewing a system orientation 
as well as receiving any basic career services available at the location they access.    Adult 
Education and Literacy has also agreed to the referral process and initial screening to identify 
potential services for all American Job Center jobseekers.    
The Adult Education and Literacy provider for Navajo and Apache Counties is Northland Pioneer 
College (NPC).  As the Title II provider, NPC provides pre-intake, registration, enrollment and 
orientation services that provide incoming students an overview of services, assists students in 
setting educational goals, and assures the completion of NPC/Title II enrollment requirements.  
At the time of the orientation, someone from Northeast Arizona Workforce Solutions is 
available to provide information regarding support services they may be able to provide.   
The College and Career Preparation (CCP) department at NPC provides educational services in 
the form of face-to-face classroom lectures, hands-on labs, online courses, and classes offered 
via the NPC distance education system.  Classes are offered in the areas of English Language

Acquisition for Adults, Civics Instruction, Adult Basic and Secondary Education, and Integrated 
Education and Technology.  Students commute to four full-service campuses, five centers, and 
numerous classroom sites throughout the service area. 
The Adult Education and Literacy provider for Gila County is located in the Comprehensive 
ARIZONA@WORK Job Center.  They are conducting GED and ESL classes, testing, and tutoring.  
A referral process is in place to capture individuals to co-enroll.

● Provide performance information as required by the Workforce Innovation and

Carl D. Perkins Career and Technical Education Act programs services are provided at Northland

The Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board has convened a Youth Program

This Ad Hoc group will continue to work toward development of strong support services

NPC Career Services hosts Northeastern Arizona Employer Outreach Meetings where both

Representatives from Adult Education and Vocational Rehabilitation sit on the Local Workforce

core partners will coordinate to transition participants into post-secondary education.   
 
Vocational Rehabilitation coordinates with Adult Education to provide transitional services.  
Specialized vocational rehabilitation counselors are assigned to work with special education 
students as they prepare to leave high school.  Vocational Rehabilitation has also a transition 
specialist in the ARIZONA@WORK Job Centers to provide services to secondary and post-

j. Transportation.  A description of how the local board will coordinate workforce 
investment activities carried out under this title in the local area with the provision 
of transportation, including public transportation, and other appropriate supportive 
services in the local area. 
 
Transportation barriers continue to be an area of concern for low-income individuals and job 
seekers with barriers to employment in Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties. 
 
Transportation is a huge barrier to employment in this large rural area comprised of the Navajo, 
Apache, and Gila Counties spanning 25,973 square miles with few public transportation systems 
serving only half of the population centers within the local area: Globe/Miami, a region of 
Navajo County spanning from Holbrook to Pinetop-Lakeside, an a newly established system 
serving Payson and Star Valley. These public transit systems have limited service hours and do 
not serve those who must arrive at work prior to 7:00 AM or after 6:00 PM.

<!-- page 425 -->

Equal Opportunity Employer/Program 
Auxiliary aids and services available upon request to individuals with disabilities  
 
The Local Workforce Development Board has approved a Supportive Services policy that 
includes transportation assistance such as auto repair, fuel cards and/or bus passes for all Title I 
participants who demonstrate a need.  The Local Workforce Development Area provides 
transportation supportive services on a case-by-case basis if assistance is required to participate 
in an education or training activity and/or seeking employment.  The lack of adequate 
transportation options throughout the entire local area was a driving force behind a supportive 
service policy that ensures participants are provided with adequate assistance in the form of 
fuel cards or bus passes to enable them to physically access employment and training 
opportunities.   
 
Clients enrolled in Vocational Rehabilitation have limited access to transportation-related 
supportive services.

a. A description of how the local board, working with the entities carrying out core 
programs, will expand access to employment, training, education, and supportive 
services for eligible individuals, particularly eligible individuals with barriers to 
employment. 
Comprehensive and Affiliate ARIZONA@WORK Job Centers are located throughout Navajo, 
Apache, and Gila Counties.  These job centers are in Globe, Payson, Winslow, Show Low, and 
Eagar and offer employers and job seekers access to information and coordinated services.  
Each job center offers access to the ARIZONA@WORK services through staff co-location and 
coordination/referral of services.  The job centers offer specialty trained staff and a variety of 
assistive technology to serve persons with disabilities. All Core Partners which include Adult, 
Dislocated Worker, and Youth Programs, Adult Education, Employment Services, and Vocational 
Rehabilitation will continually collaborate and coordinate with each other to provide services to 
individuals with barriers to employment.  
 
ARIZONA@WORK Job Centers connect individuals to multiple partners to develop an 
employment plan for skill gaps and barriers to employment as defined by the partners.  The 
partners will collaborate within the employment plan to overcome these barriers to obtain 
unsubsidized employment.   
 
The Local Workforce Development Board has established a Priority of Services to ensure the 
delivery of career and training services for the Adult, Youth, Veteran, and eligible spouses of 
Veterans that are most in need.   
 
The Local Workforce Development Board will continue to pursue strategies to improve 
availability and coordination of ARIZONA@WORK System Services to employers and job 
seekers. The LWDB significantly revised the scope of work and available funding during its 
procurement of a new One-Stop Operator in November 2018 to greatly enhance the level of 
system coordination and add community-based partners to meet the needs of all job seekers in 
the large and rural local area. This will enhance the Local Workforce Development Area’s ability 
to carry out the vision and goals for the workforce system: 
 
● Ensure that ALL jobseeker clients, regardless of where they first access the workforce 
system, will be registered in the ARIZONA@WORK database.  
● Establish a welcome and intake process that includes initial screening questions to 
identify potential partner engagement. 
● Establish partner lists of typical characteristics for each program regarding potential 
eligibility and suitability for enrollment to assist with targeted referrals.     
● Partners will continue to enhance the referral process based on the initial screening 
findings and has agreed that if the initial screening is done at the Job Centers, basic 
individualized services will be offered and provided by all co-located partners.

Access to services will be achieved through a variety of mechanisms starting with training all

providing workforce system initial screening questions, and offering the system-wide 
orientation that is being developed.   This “no wrong door” concept allows multiple and varied 
locations.

The LWDB has recently implemented a cloud- based Client Referral System that is utilized by all 
core program partners. The One -Stop Operator works with ARIZONA@WORK and community 
partners to  gain access to and fully utilize the features of the CRS to ensure job seekers are 
receiving necessary services. The LWDB is currently exploring options for expanding on the Client 
Referral System or purchasing a system that would allow for common intake among core, 
required, and community partners to support a comprehensive intake and case management 
system that would also incorporate communication between partners offering wrap -around 
services to support the needs of all job seekers. Similarly, the LWDB has begun the process to 
develop a region-wide formal business services team and is exploring technology-based solutions 
to enhance communication regarding employer outreach efforts and increasing the efficacy of 
recruiting employers in supporting and utilizing the ARIZONA@WORK system. 
 
The LWDB is currently researching additional technology solutions that will increase jobseeker 
access to ARIZONA@WORK services in all parts of the LWDA. For example, the LWDB is working 
with community organizations throughout Navajo, Apache,  and Gila Counties to develop the 
Northeastern Arizona Community Resource Network (NACRN) that will include the creation of a 
web portal that all NACRN partners can utilize as a common intake and referral system. This will 
increase the num ber of access points where job seekers can receive information and a direct 
connection to the ARIZONA@WORK Northeastern Arizona One-Stop System.  
     
Technology is encouraged throughout the Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act.   
Information will be provided on all core program partner websites on services that are available 
via the web.    Information on how to access web resources will be provided via the system 
orientation and through outreach and recruitment materials. Technology use will be enhanced 
as job seekers access on-line assessments and learning opportunities.  such as the Arizona 
Career Readiness Credential.     
 
Social media will continue to play an increasing role in the delivery of services.  The use of 
Facebook text messaging and Twitter increases case management services and provides 
additional means of disseminating information about services available to job seekers and 
employers through the ARIZONA@WORK Northeastern Arizona system.   
 
Due to the rural nature of the Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties, direct service delivery staff for

such as educational and employment outcome, which includes Arizona Job Connection, DES 
GUIDE, and Experian Work Number.

The Local Workforce Development Area has developed and maintains a reasonable

The Local Workforce Development Board adopted an Equal Opportunity and Nondiscrimination 
Policy, WIA Administration Procedure #2010-002 Equal Opportunity and Nondiscrimination 
Policy.  The Local Workforce Development Area will comply with the State’s Nondiscrimination 
Plan in each of the ARIZONA@WORK Job Centers. 
 
The State’s Nondiscrimination Plan is comprised of nine elements that describe the actions the 
Local Workforce Development Board has taken and will continue to take to ensure that its 
WIOA Title 1-financially assisted programs, activities, and recipients are complying, and will 
continue to comply, with the nondiscrimination and equal opportunity requirements of WIOA 
and regulations. 
 
The LWDB is reviewing the following policies for implementation to ensure compliance with 
non-discrimination: 
Designation of Local Level Equal Opportunity Officer: The Equal Opportunity Officer was 
designated by the Local Workforce Development Board.  Notification of the EO Officers’ 
identities are posted on the “Equal Opportunity is the Law” posters in the lobby and 
ARIZONA@WORK Job Centers in both English and Spanish plus a copy is given to each WIOA 
participant.  This signed copy is place in the participants file. 
 
Notice and Communication: The Local Workforce Development Board has established a notice 
and communication system that is accessible to all registrants, applicants, eligible 
applicants/registrants, applicants for employment, employees and interested members of the 
public, making them aware of their obligation to operate programs and activities in a 
nondiscriminatory manner, and the extent of the rights of members of these protected groups 
to file complaints of discrimination. 
 
Applicants, participants, other recipients, members of the public and individuals with 
disabilities are notified of the procedures to request accommodations. Accommodations are 
available upon requests in all locations. 
 
Review Assurances, Job Training Plans, Contract and Policies and Procedures: The Local 
Workforce Development Board has incorporated the language required for Equal Opportunity 
(EO) and Affirmative Action into all its contracts and agreements and provides assurance for

Equal Opportunity Employment Policy 
 
The Local Workforce Development Board has an Equal Opportunity Employment Policy 
in compliance with Section 188(a)(1) and (2) which ensures that no individual shall be 
subjected to discrimination relating to employment in the administration of or in 
connection with any such program under this title. The LWDB works closely with its 
various partners to ensure that services are delivered on a non-discriminatory basis. 
 
If a complaint is filed with the State of Arizona, the individual must wait either until the 
State of Arizona issues a written Notice of Final Action, or until 90 days have passed, 
(whichever is sooner), before filing a complaint with the Civil Rights Center.    If the State 
of Arizona does not give the individual a written Notice of Final Action within 90 days of 
the day on which the complaint was file, the individual does not have to wait for the 
State of Arizona to issue that Notice before filing a complaint with the Civil Rights 
Center.   However, the individual must file the Civil Rights Complaint within 30 days of 
the 90-day deadline (in other words, within 120 days after the day on which the

<!-- page 434 -->
42 
Equal Opportunity Employer/Program 
Auxiliary aids and services available upon request to individuals with disabilities  
complaint was filed with the State of Arizona).   If the State of Arizona gives the 
individual a written Notice of Final Action on the complaint, but the individual is 
dissatisfied with the decision or resolution, the individual may file a complaint with the 
Civil Rights Center.    The individual must file the Civil Rights Center complaint within 30 
days of the date on which the individual received the Notice of Final Action. 
 
All complaints will be logged and timeframes documented to ensure compliance with 
the law, regulations, and policies.

Section 7:  Employer Engagement/ 
Coordination with Economic Development 
 Provide a description of how the local board/local area will: 
 a. Coordinate workforce investment activities carried out in the local area with 
economic development activities carried out in the region in which the local area is 
located. 
Navajo County combined the Economic and Workforce Development Departments in 2017 and 
the Director and Assistant Director conduct both economic and workforce development activities 
concurrently, being mindful of the region’s unique needs and the inherent overlap of activities. 
This merging of departments has greatly increased communication and collaboration between 
economic development and workforce development and has created an environment where 
workforce development is at the table during regional planning meetings and is heavily involved 
in efforts to diversify and strengthen the economy. This has also increased the collaboration 
between economic development and regional training providers to ensure that training that is 
available in the region meets the needs of current  employers as well as businesses that are 
considering expanding or relocating to the region. 
 
Economic Development has participated in the Local Workforce Development Board to discuss 
and formulate strategies to serve business and industry effectively and played an important 
advisory role in the development of the local plan including input regarding existing and 
emerging industries and occupations that are forecasted to be in-demand during the upcoming 
four years.    The Local Workforce Development Board and Economic Development have agreed 
to participate in collecting information from business customers regarding the skills and 
knowledge they are looking for to fill entry level and middle skill jobs in the in-demand 
occupations.   
b. Focus on sector strategies (include a description); include statewide sectors that 
exist in the local area plus local area specific sectors. 
Core Program Partners, Gila Community College, Northland Pioneer College, and Economic 
Development will assist in the support of sector relationships for the identified existing and 
emerging industries and occupations.    
 
The sector relationships will incorporate career pathways into its responsibilities in terms of 
creating a flow of the career path options, analysis of the needs and expectations of business 
customers, analysis of the existing skills and knowledge of the talent pipeline, and work with 
education representatives to tap into existing training or create new credentialing or 
certification options.    
 
The Local Workforce Development Board has identified Healthcare, Construction, Utilities, 
Manufacturing, and Transportation sectors and associated career pathways in Navajo,

Apache, and Gila Counties.  In partnership with Local Workforce Development Board 
Members, Employers, and Community Colleges, workgroups will be formed to focus on the 
workforce needs within each sector.  The Local Workforce Development Board will take a 
lead role in organizing the sector workgroups. 
 
The Local Workforce Board has targeted two areas that are aligned with the state’s selected 
industry sectors: Advanced Manufacturing and Health Care. These areas are compatible with 
the Local Workforce Development Board’s existing and emerging industries and growth 
occupations.  
c. Facilitate engagement of employers, including small employers and employers in 
in-demand industry sectors and occupations, in workforce development programs. 
LWDB Members and Staff are active in regional business associations such as the Chambers of 
Commerce and Economic Development Councils and regularly make presentations regarding the 
Workforce Development Board to recruit new members. The LWDB has also  recently convened 
a Board Recruitment and Engagement Work Group and has developed a Board Member 
Overview and Job Description that current LWDB members can use as a recruitment tool when 
conducting outreach to business colleagues. 
 
Through relationships with employers, local business intermediaries including the Chambers of 
Commerce, economic development entities, and ARIZONA@WORK partners, the LWDB is 
continually developing and increasing effective linkages to employers in the region. Board 
members and st aff actively participate in health care, manufacturing, and utilities advisory 
meetings and partner with the local community colleges to sponsor job fairs and engage in a 
variety of outreach activities to increase employer awareness, use, and support of lo cal 
workforce investment activities. During PY17, a total of 453 staff -assisted job orders were 
entered in Globe, Show Low, Winslow, Eagar, and Payson. 
 
The Local Workforce Development Board will continue its outreach and relationship- building 
with local businesses through partnership with Economic Development, Chambers of 
Commerce, and industry associations.  Business customers play a key role in the success of 
the Workforce Development System. 
The Local Workforce Development Area partners will make concerted efforts to identify, 
understand and respond to the specific needs of its current and future employers. We will: 
 
● Outreach to small and large employers and In-Demand industries 
● Targeted visits to various community meetings, associations, chambers 
● Outreach to colleges 
● Design training in concert with Community Colleges for the purposes of career pathways 
that provide skilled workers based on employer need.

d. Support a local workforce development system that meets the needs of businesses 
in the local area e.That may include the implementation of initiatives such as 
incumbent worker training programs, on-the-job training programs, customized 
training programs, industry and sector strategies, career pathways initiatives, 
utilization of effective business intermediaries, and other business services and 
strategies, designed to meet the needs of employers in the corresponding region in 
support of the strategy described in (1)(F). 
 
The merger of the Navajo County Economic and Workforce Development Departments has led 
to promising strategies for increasing communication to and from employers and working with 
training providers to increase the validity of training programs to meet the needs of employers. 
The LWDB has worked closely with the State Office of Appr enticeship to develop health care 
apprenticeships that will expand employment and career advancement opportunities in health 
care occupations. Entrepreneurship is a focus of economic diversification efforts and the LWDB 
is partnering with economic developm ent entities to develop a business incubator and 
entrepreneurship development/mentorship program that will include those individuals with 
barriers to employment, particularly the out-of-school youth demographic.  
 
Employer needs and expectations will be established through the work of our sector 
relationships along with recommended career pathways flow for each industry and occupation.   
Economic development partners in the Local Workforce Development Area have agreed to 
assist with the sector relationships and gather information on the needs and expectations of 
business and industry.   Existing and emerging industries and occupations representatives who 
are Local Workforce Development Board Members will participate on the appropriate sector 
discussions along with union representatives and educational institutions.   
   
Successful workforce systems utilize both job development and business development methods 
in serving business and industry customers and assisting jobseekers in getting and keeping 
employment.  It is understood that job development and business development are very 
different outreach and recruitment approaches to working with business and industry.     
 
The Local Workforce Development Board sees job development as an approach that requires 
specific funding sources to reach out to small and family-owned businesses with a particular 
client in mind in an effort to place the individual with known barriers, strengths, and 
weaknesses in a work-based learning opportunity.    Job development will remain the 
responsibility and role of individual partner programs but will be coordinated on a system-wide 
level through the creation of the Business Services team to continue to ensure employers have 
a single point of contact regardless of the specific ARIZONA@WORK Northeastern Arizona 
services utilized by each employer.   
 
Business development, on the other hand, is about outreach and recruitment of business 
customers to request applicants when openings occur.   Typically, business development is 
medium size to large businesses.   The Local Workforce Development Board will form an

<!-- page 438 -->
46

integrated Business Service Team to conduct business development.   The One-Stop Operator 
will act as the convener and coordinator of this Business Service Team and has been tasked 
with reporting to the LWDB on the progress of the entire Business Service Team and individual 
coalitions or community groups that are formed to specifically meet the needs of each unique 
population center within the local area. 
    
The One-Stop Operator will work with all members of the  Business Service Team, which will 
include Local Workforce Development Board members and staff, core program partners as well 
as Economic Development, educational service providers, local employers, and community-
based organizations, to  align and integrate business development activities within the Local 
Development Area for the purpose of: 
 
● Creating a single point of contact for businesses 
● Establishing a collaborative outreach and recruitment structure  
● Coordinating workforce services and training options 
● Leveraging resources  
● Sharing performance outcomes 
● Creating and managing a talent pipeline in existing and emerging in-demand industries 
and occupations 
● Establishing an inventory of existing training options, particularly those that include a 
credential or certification in existing and emerging in-demand industries and 
occupations 
● Identifying skill and knowledge gaps between what business needs and expects and 
what training options are available 
● Working with Education to enhance or establish short-term training options for portable 
or stackable credential/certification opportunities 
● Working with Economic Development Representatives to ensure a dynamic approach to 
meeting the ever-changing needs of business and industry as the in-demand sector 
landscape grows
 or diminishes 
 
f.Strengthen linkages between the one-stop delivery system and unemployment insurance 
programs. 
The Comprehensive ARIZONA@WORK Job Center staff assists Unemployment Insurance 
claimants in creating individual reemployment plans based on current labor market 
information. Unemployment Insurance Claimants are required to register in the Arizona Job 
Connection database. The staff ensures that claimants are engaging in regular job search 
efforts to obtain work.  Staff provides a series of job search workshops that are designed to 
facilitate the job seeker’s success. Comprehensive and collaborative services to job seekers 
through the ARIZONA@WORK Job Center System include referrals, job applications, and 
assistance job openings, training and education programs. The claimants who are most likely 
to exhaust benefits before finding employment are targeted for more skills development.
